| w _ {1, 1} | > \frac {\left(| z | - \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}\right) \left(| z ^ {\prime} | - \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}\right)}{| \epsilon | + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}} \geq \frac {| z | \cdot | z ^ {\prime} |}{| \epsilon | + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}} - \left(| z | + | z ^ {\prime} |\right).
$$

![](images/64ad339f6e6f4ed317730111a614787e3aae69341c9e1a93b18ab6b327ee64ef.jpg)

We are now able to see that, indeed, the smaller  $|\epsilon|$  is compared to  $|z \cdot z'|$ , the higher  $|w_{1,1}|$  will be driven when the loss is minimized. With Lemma 25 in place, we are now able to bound the singular values of  $W_{L:1}$ .

Lemma 26. The singular values of  $W_{L:1}$  fulfill:

$$
\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) \geq \frac {1}{\sqrt {2}} \cdot | w _ {1, 1} | - \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)},
$$

$$
\sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) \leq 4 | \epsilon | + \left(4 + \frac {\sqrt {| z | \cdot | z ^ {\prime} |}}{\min  \{| z | , | z ^ {\prime} | \}}\right) \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}. \tag {49}
$$

Furthermore, if  $\ell(W_{L:1}) < \min\{z^2/2, z'^2/2\}$ , the bound on  $\sigma_2(W_{L:1})$  may be simplified:

$$
\sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) \leq 4 | \epsilon | + 4 \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}. \tag {50}
$$

Proof. Define  $W_{\widetilde{S}} \coloneqq \left( \begin{array}{cc} w_{1,1} & z' \\ z & \epsilon \end{array} \right)$ , the orthogonal projection of  $W_{L:1}$  onto the solution set  $\widetilde{S}$ . From Corollary 8.6.2 in [33] we know that:

$$
\left| \sigma_ {i} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) - \sigma_ {i} \left(W _ {\tilde {\mathcal {S}}}\right) \right| \leq \left\| W _ {L: 1} - W _ {\tilde {\mathcal {S}}} \right\| _ {F} = \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)} \quad , i = 1, 2. \tag {51}
$$

This means that any bound on the singular values of  $W_{\widetilde{S}}$  can be transferred to those of  $W_{L:1}$  (up to an additive loss-dependent term). It is straightforwardly verified that the squared singular values of  $W_{\widetilde{S}}$  are

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {1} ^ {2} (W _ {\widetilde {S}}) = \frac {1}{2} \left(w _ {1, 1} ^ {2} + z ^ {2} + z ^ {\prime 2} + \epsilon^ {2} + \sqrt {\left(w _ {1 , 1} ^ {2} + z ^ {2} + z ^ {\prime 2} + \epsilon^ {2}\right) ^ {2} - 4 \left(w _ {1 , 1} \epsilon - z z ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}}\right), \\ \sigma_ {2} ^ {2} (W _ {\mathcal {\tilde {S}}}) = \frac {1}{2} \left(w _ {1, 1} ^ {2} + z ^ {2} + z ^ {\prime 2} + \epsilon^ {2} - \sqrt {\left(w _ {1 , 1} ^ {2} + z ^ {2} + z ^ {\prime 2} + \epsilon^ {2}\right) ^ {2} - 4 \left(w _ {1 , 1} \epsilon - z z ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Note that the term inside the square roots is non-negative for all  $w_{1,1}, z, z', \epsilon$ . Since all elements in the expression for  $\sigma_1^2(W_{\widetilde{S}})$  are non-negative, we have  $\sigma_1(W_{\widetilde{S}}) \geq (1/\sqrt{2}) \cdot |w_{1,1}|$ . Combining this with Equation (51) completes the lower bound for  $\sigma_1(W_{L:1})$ .

Next, let  $W_{\widetilde{S}_0} \coloneqq \left( \begin{array}{cc} w_{1,1} & z' \\ z & 0 \end{array} \right)$  be the matrix obtained by replacing the bottom-right entry of  $W_{\widetilde{S}}$  by 0. Replacing  $\epsilon$  with 0 in Equation (52), and applying the identity  $a - b = \frac{a^2 - b^2}{a + b}$  for  $a, b \in \mathbb{R}$  such that  $a + b \neq 0$ , we get:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {2} ^ {2} \left(W _ {\widetilde {S} _ {0}}\right) = \frac {2 z ^ {2} z ^ {\prime 2}}{w _ {1 , 1} ^ {2} + z ^ {2} + z ^ {\prime 2} + \sqrt {\left(w _ {1 , 1} ^ {2} + z ^ {2} + z ^ {\prime 2}\right) ^ {2} - 4 z ^ {2} z ^ {\prime 2}}} \tag {53} \\ \leq \frac {2 z ^ {2} z ^ {\prime 2}}{w _ {1 , 1} ^ {2} + z ^ {2} + z ^ {\prime 2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

We initially prove Equation (50) holds in the case where  $\ell(W_{L:1}) < \min\{z^2/2, z'^2/2\}$ . By lifting said assumption we then show that the bound on  $\sigma_2(W_{L:1})$  in Equation (49) holds for any loss value. Under the assumption that  $\ell(W_{L:1}) < \min\{z^2/2, z'^2/2\}$ , taking the square root of both sides in Equation (53), we arrive at the following bound:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {\tilde {\mathcal {G}} _ {0}}\right) \leq \sqrt {2} \cdot \frac {| z | \cdot | z ^ {\prime} |}{\sqrt {w _ {1 , 1} ^ {2} + z ^ {2} + z ^ {\prime 2}}} \\ \leq \sqrt {6} \cdot \frac {| z | \cdot | z ^ {\prime} |}{| w _ {1 , 1} | + | z | + | z ^ {\prime} |} \\ \leq \sqrt {6} \cdot \frac {| z | \cdot | z ^ {\prime} |}{\frac {| z | \cdot | z ^ {\prime} |}{| \epsilon | + \sqrt {2 \ell (W _ {L : 1})}}} \\ \leq 3 \left(| \epsilon | + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the second transition we applied the inequality  $\sqrt{w_{1,1}^2 + z^2 + z'^2} \geq (|w_{1,1}| + |z| + |z'|) / \sqrt{3}$ , and in the third made use of the bound on  $|w_{1,1}|$  (Lemma 25). Applying Corollary 8.6.2 from [33] twice, once for the matrices  $W_{L:1}$  and  $W_{\widetilde{S}}$ , and another for  $W_{\widetilde{S}}$  and  $W_{\widetilde{S}_0}$ , we have:

$$
\sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) \leq 3 \left(\left| \epsilon \right| + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}\right) + \left| \epsilon \right| + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)} = 4 \left(\left| \epsilon \right| + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}\right),
$$

achieving the desired result from Equation (50). It remains to see that the bound on  $\sigma_{2}(W_{L:1})$  in Equation (49) holds regardless of the loss value. When  $\ell(W_{L:1}) < \min\{z^2/2, z'^2/2\}$  it obviously holds since it is only looser than the bound already obtained under this assumption. Otherwise, going back to Equation (53), it can be seen that

$$
\sigma_ {2} ^ {2} \left(W _ {\widetilde {\mathcal {S}} _ {0}}\right) \leq \frac {2 z ^ {2} z ^ {\prime 2}}{\left(z - z ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} + 2 | z | \cdot | z ^ {\prime} |} \leq | z | \cdot | z ^ {\prime} |.
$$

Thus,  $\sigma_{2}(W_{\widetilde{S}_{0}}) \leq \sqrt{|z| \cdot |z'|}$ . Following the same procedure as before (applying Corollary 8.6.2 from [33]), combined with the fact that  $\ell(W_{L:1}) \geq \min \left\{z^{2}/2, z'^{2}/2\right\}$  concludes the proof:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) \leq \sqrt {\left| z \right| \cdot \left| z ^ {\prime} \right|} + \left| \epsilon \right| + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)} \\ \leq \frac {\sqrt {| z | \cdot | z ^ {\prime} |}}{\min \{| z | , | z ^ {\prime} | \}} \cdot \sqrt {2 \ell (W _ {L : 1})} + | \epsilon | + \sqrt {2 \ell (W _ {L : 1})} \\ \leq 4 \left| \epsilon \right| + \left(4 + \frac {\sqrt {| z | \cdot | z ^ {\prime} |}}{\operatorname* {m i n} \{| z | , | z ^ {\prime} | \}}\right) \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/54a033ccc7a50c48d478edb5c8bb8e2dfc5121537f70cdeff854f851e7ee6778.jpg)

# D.8.1 Proof of Equation (13) (lower bound for quasi-norm)

Turning our attention to  $\| W_{L:1}\|$ , following the same steps as in the proof of Theorem 1 (Subappendix D.5.1) will lead to a generalized bound. By the triangle inequality:

$$
\left\| W _ {L: 1} \right\| \geq \frac {1}{c _ {\| \cdot \|}} \left\| w _ {1, 1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} ^ {\top} \right\| - \left\| W _ {L: 1} - w _ {1, 1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} ^ {\top} \right\|, \tag {54}
$$

where  $c_{\| \cdot \|}\geq 1$  is a constant with which  $\| \cdot \|$  satisfies the weakened triangle inequality (see Footnote 2). Let us initially assume that  $\ell (W_{L:1}) < \min \{z^2 /2,z'^2 /2\}$ . We later lift this assumption, delivering a bound that holds for all loss values. Invoking Equation (48) we may bound the negative term in Equation (54) as follows:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| W _ {L: 1} - w _ {1, 1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} ^ {\top} \right\| \leq c _ {\| \cdot \|} | w _ {2, 2} | \left\| \mathbf {e} _ {2} \mathbf {e} _ {2} ^ {\top} \right\| + c _ {\| \cdot \|} ^ {2} \left(| w _ {2, 1} | \left\| \mathbf {e} _ {2} \mathbf {e} _ {1} ^ {\top} \right\| + | w _ {1, 2} | \left\| \mathbf {e} _ {1} \mathbf {e} _ {2} ^ {\top} \right\|\right) \\ \leq 3c_{\| \cdot \|}^{2}\left(\max \{|z|,|z^{\prime}|,|\epsilon |\} +\sqrt{2\ell(W_{L:1})}\right)\max_{\substack{i,j\in \{1,2\} \\ (i,j)\neq (1,1)}}\bigl\|\mathbf{e}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\bigr\| \\ \leq 6c_{\| \cdot \|}^{2}\max \{|z|,|z^{\prime}|,|\epsilon |\} \cdot \max_{\substack{i,j\in \{1,2\} \\ (i,j)\neq (1,1)}}\bigl|\mathbf{e}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\bigr|\bigl|  , \\ \end{array}
$$

Returning to Equation (54), applying the inequality above and the bound on  $|w_{1,1}|$  (Lemma 25) we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| W_{L:1}\| \geq \frac{\left\|\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\right\|}{c_{\|\cdot\|}}\left(\frac{|z|\cdot|z^{\prime}|}{|\epsilon| + \sqrt{2\ell(W_{L:1})}} -|z| - |z^{\prime}|\right) - 6c_{\|\cdot\|}^{2}\max \{|z|,|z^{\prime}|,|\epsilon |\} \max_{\substack{i,j\in \{1,2\} \\ (i,j)\neq (1,1)}}\left\| \mathbf{e}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\right\| \\ \geq \frac {\left\| \mathbf {e} _ {1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} ^ {\top} \right\|}{c _ {\| \cdot \|}} \cdot \frac {| z | \cdot | z ^ {\prime} |}{| \epsilon | + \sqrt {2 \ell (W _ {L : 1})}} - 8 c _ {\| \cdot \|} ^ {2} \max \{| z |, | z ^ {\prime} |, | \epsilon | \} \cdot \max _ {i, j \in \{1, 2 \}} \left\| \mathbf {e} _ {i} \mathbf {e} _ {j} ^ {\top} \right\|. \\ \end{array}
$$

Since  $\| W_{L:1} \|$  is trivially lower bounded by zero, defining the constants

$$
a _ {\| \cdot \|} := \frac {\left\| \mathbf {e} _ {1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} ^ {\top} \right\|}{c _ {\| \cdot \|}}, b _ {\| \cdot \|} := \max  \left\{\frac {a _ {\| \cdot \|} \cdot | z | \cdot | z ^ {\prime} |}{| \epsilon | + \min  \{| z | , | z ^ {\prime} | \}}, 8 c _ {\| \cdot \|} ^ {2} \max  \{| z |, | z ^ {\prime} |, | \epsilon | \} \max  _ {i, j \in \{1, 2 \}} \left\| \mathbf {e} _ {i} \mathbf {e} _ {j} ^ {\top} \right\| \right\},
$$

allows us, on the one hand, to arrive at a bound of the form:

$$
\left\| W _ {L: 1} \right\| \geq a _ {\parallel \cdot \parallel} \cdot \frac {| z | \cdot | z ^ {\prime} |}{| \epsilon | + \sqrt {2 \ell (W _ {L : 1})}} - b _ {\parallel \cdot \parallel},
$$

and on the other hand, to remove the previous assumption on the loss: in the case where  $\ell(W_{L:1}) \geq \min\{z^2/2, z'^2/2\}$ , the bound is non-positive and trivially holds. Noticing this is exactly Equation (13) (recall we omitted the time index  $t$ ), concludes this part of the proof.

# D.8.2 Proof of Equation (14) (upper bound for effective rank)

Derivation of the upper bound for effective rank (Definition 1) is initially done under the assumption that  $\ell(W_{L:1}) < \min\{z^2/8, z'^2/8\}$ . We then remove this assumption, establishing a bound that holds for all loss values.

The bounds on  $\sigma_{1}(W_{L:1})$  and  $\sigma_{2}(W_{L:1})$  in Lemma 26 give:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \rho_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) = \frac {\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}{\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L : 1}\right) + \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)} \\ \geq \frac {\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}{\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L : 1}\right) + 4 \left(\left| \epsilon \right| + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}\right)} \\ = 1 - \frac {4 \left(| \epsilon | + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}\right)}{\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L : 1}\right) + 4 \left(| \epsilon | + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}\right)} \\ \geq 1 - \frac {4 \left(\left| \epsilon \right| + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}\right)}{\frac {1}{\sqrt {2}} \cdot \left| w _ {1 , 1} \right| + 4 \left| \epsilon \right| + 3 \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}} \\ \geq 1 - \frac {4 \sqrt {2} \left(| \epsilon | + \sqrt {2 \ell (W _ {L : 1})}\right)}{| w _ {1 , 1} |}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Additionally, under our assumption that  $\ell(W_{L:1}) < \min\{z^2/8, z'^2/8\}$ , the bound on  $|w_{1,1}|$  in Lemma 25 can be simplified to:

$$
\left| w _ {1, 1} \right| \geq \frac {\left(\left| z \right| - \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}\right) \left(\left| z ^ {\prime} \right| - \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}\right)}{\left| \epsilon \right| + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}} \geq \frac {\min  \left\{\left| z \right| , \left| z ^ {\prime} \right| \right\} ^ {2}}{4 \left(\left| \epsilon \right| + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}\right)}.
$$

Combining the last two inequalities we have:

$$
\rho_ {2} (W _ {L: 1}) = 1 - \rho_ {1} (W _ {L: 1}) \leq \frac {1 6 \sqrt {2} \left(| \epsilon | + \sqrt {2 \ell (W _ {L : 1})}\right) ^ {2}}{\min \{| z | , | z ^ {\prime} | \} ^ {2}}.
$$

It is now possible to see that, in accordance with Subsection 4.4, the smaller  $|\epsilon|$  is compared to  $\min \{|z|, |z'|\}$ , the closer to zero  $\rho_2(W_{L:1})$  becomes as the loss is minimized. Let  $h(\rho_2(W_{L:1})) := -\rho_2(W_{L:1}) \cdot \ln(\rho_2(W_{L:1})) - (1 - \rho_2(W_{L:1})) \cdot \ln(1 - \rho_2(W_{L:1}))$  denote the binary entropy function, and recall that the effective rank of the product matrix defined to be  $\mathrm{erank}(W_{L:1}) := \exp\{h(\rho_2(W_{L:1}))\}$ . As in the proof of Theorem 1 (Subappendix D.5.2), we may bound the exponent on the interval  $[0, \ln(2)]$  by the linear function intersecting it at these points. That is,

$$
\operatorname {e r a n k} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) \leq 1 + \frac {1}{\ln (2)} \cdot h \left(\rho_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right)\right).
$$

From Lemma 9 it holds that  $h\left(\rho_{2}\left(W_{L:1}\right)\right) \leq 2\sqrt{\rho_{2}\left(W_{L:1}\right)}$ . Plugging this into the inequality above leads to:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {e r a n k} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) \leq 1 + \frac {8 \cdot 2 ^ {\frac {1}{4}}}{\ln (2) \cdot \min  \{| z | , | z ^ {\prime} | \}} \cdot \left(| \epsilon | + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}\right) \\ \leq 1 + \frac {1 6}{\operatorname* {m i n} \{| z | , | z ^ {\prime} | \}} \cdot \left(| \epsilon | + \sqrt {2 \ell (W _ {L : 1})}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second transition is a slight simplification of the constants  $(2^{1/4} / \ln(2) < 2)$ . As will be shown below,  $\inf_{W' \in \widetilde{S}} \operatorname{erank}(W') = 1$ . We may thus conclude:

$$
\operatorname {e r a n k} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) \leq \inf  _ {W ^ {\prime} \in \widetilde {S}} \operatorname {e r a n k} \left(W ^ {\prime}\right) + \frac {1 6}{\min  \{| z | , | z ^ {\prime} | \}} \cdot \left(| \epsilon | + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}\right).
$$

Notice that when  $\ell(W_{L:1}) \geq \min\{z^2/8, z'^2/8\}$  the inequality trivially holds since the right-hand side is greater than 2 (the maximal effective rank for a  $2 \times 2$  matrix). This establishes Equation (14) (time index is omitted).

It remains to prove that  $\inf_{W' \in \widetilde{S}} \operatorname{erank}(W') = 1$ . If  $\epsilon \neq 0$ , it is trivial since there exists  $W' \in \widetilde{S}$  with  $\operatorname{rank}(W') = 1$ , meaning  $\sigma_2(W') = 0$  and  $\operatorname{erank}(W') = 1$ . If  $\epsilon = 0$ , examining the

squared singular values of  $W' \in \widetilde{S}$  (Equation (52) with  $(W')_{1,1}$  in place of  $w_{1,1}$ ) reveals that  $\lim_{(W')_{1,1} \to \infty} \sigma_2(W') = 0$ , while  $\lim_{(W')_{1,1} \to \infty} \sigma_1(W') = \infty$ . Thus, there exists a matrix in  $\widetilde{S}$  with effective rank arbitrarily close to 1. Since the effective rank of any matrix is at least 1, this implies that  $\inf_{W' \in \widetilde{S}} \operatorname{erank}(W') = 1$ .

# D.8.3 Proof of Equation (15) (upper bound for distance from infimal rank)

We claim that the infimal rank (Definition 2) of  $\widetilde{S}$  is 1. Since  $z, z' \neq 0$ , it cannot be 0. If  $\epsilon \neq 0$ , our claim is trivial since there exists  $W' \in \widetilde{S}$  with  $\mathrm{rank}(W') = 1$ . Otherwise, inspecting the squared singular values of a matrix  $W' \in \widetilde{S}$  (Equation (52) with  $(W')_{1,1}$  in place of  $w_{1,1}$ ), we can see that, when  $\epsilon = 0$ , taking  $(W')_{1,1}$  to infinity drives the minimal singular value towards zero  $(\lim_{(W')_{1,1} \to \infty} \sigma_2(W') = 0)$ . Hence, the distance of  $\widetilde{S}$  from the set of matrices with rank 1 or less is 0 in this case as well.

The distance of the product matrix from the infimal rank of  $\widetilde{S}$  is therefore  $D(W_{L:1}(t),\mathcal{M}_1) = \sigma_2(W_{L:1}(t))$ . From Lemma 26 we have

$$
D \left(W _ {L: 1} (t), \mathcal {M} _ {1}\right) \leq 4 | \epsilon | + \left(4 + \frac {\sqrt {| z | \cdot | z ^ {\prime} |}}{\min  \{| z | , | z ^ {\prime} | \}}\right) \sqrt {2 \ell (t)},
$$

for all  $t\geq 0$

# D.8.4 Robustness to change in observed locations

Lastly, we prove that the established bounds (Equations (13), (14) and (15)) are robust to a change in observed locations. Let  $(i,j)\in [2]\times [2]$  be the unobserved entry's location. Following proof steps analogous to those in Lemmas 25 and 26 — while recalling our assumption of  $\operatorname*{det}(W_{L:1}(0))$  having same sign as  $z\cdot z^{\prime}$  if  $i = j$  and opposite sign otherwise — yields identical bounds on the unobserved entry and singular values of  $W_{L:1}$ . Since the derivations of Equations (13), (14) and (15) in Subappendices D.8.1, D.8.2 and D.8.3, respectively, rely solely on the aforementioned bounds, the proof concludes.

# D.9 Proof of Lemma 1

For  $l \in [L]$ , let  $W_{l} = U_{l}\Sigma_{l}V_{l}^{\top}$  be a singular value decomposition of  $W_{l}$ , i.e.  $U_{l}, V_{l} \in \mathbb{R}^{d,d}$  are orthogonal matrices, and  $\Sigma_{l} \in \mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{d,d}$  is diagonal holding the singular values of  $W_{l}$  in non-increasing order. Define  $\{W_{l}' \in \mathbb{R}^{d,d}\}_{l=1}^{L}$  by  $W_{1}' := W_{1}$ , and:

$$
W _ {l} ^ {\prime} := \prod_ {2} ^ {r = l} \left[ U _ {r} V _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] \cdot U _ {1} \Sigma_ {1} U _ {1} ^ {\top} \cdot \prod_ {r = 2} ^ {l - 1} \left[ V _ {r} U _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] \quad ,   l = 2, 3, \ldots , L.
$$

First, for  $l \in [L - 1]$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} W _ {l + 1} ^ {\prime \top} W _ {l + 1} ^ {\prime} = \prod_ {2} ^ {r = l} \left[ U _ {r} V _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] U _ {1} \Sigma_ {1} U _ {1} ^ {\top} \prod_ {r = 2} ^ {l + 1} \left[ V _ {r} U _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] \cdot \prod_ {2} ^ {r = l + 1} \left[ U _ {r} V _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] U _ {1} \Sigma_ {1} U _ {1} ^ {\top} \prod_ {r = 2} ^ {l} \left[ V _ {r} U _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] \\ = \prod_ {2} ^ {r = l} \left[ U _ {r} V _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] U _ {1} \Sigma_ {1} U _ {1} ^ {\top} \prod_ {r = 2} ^ {l - 1} \left[ V _ {r} U _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] \cdot \prod_ {2} ^ {r = l - 1} \left[ U _ {r} V _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] U _ {1} \Sigma_ {1} U _ {1} ^ {\top} \prod_ {r = 2} ^ {l} \left[ V _ {r} U _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] \\ = W _ {l} ^ {\prime} W _ {l} ^ {\prime \top}, \\ \end{array}
$$

i.e.  $W_1', W_2', \ldots, W_L'$  are balanced.

Second, by induction over  $l \in [L]$ , we prove that  $\| W_{l} - W_{l}^{\prime}\|_{F} \leq (l - 1) \cdot \sqrt{\epsilon}$ . For  $l = 1$  this is trivial, as  $W_{1}^{\prime} = W_{1}$  by definition. Assume that the bound holds for all  $j < l$ . Expressing  $W_{l}$  and  $W_{l}^{\prime}$  in terms of  $\{U_r, V_r, \Sigma_r\}_{r=1}^l$  yields:

$$
\| W _ {l} - W _ {l} ^ {\prime} \| _ {F} = \left\| U _ {l} \Sigma_ {l} V _ {l} ^ {\top} - \prod_ {2} ^ {r = l} \left[ U _ {r} V _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] \cdot U _ {1} \Sigma_ {1} U _ {1} ^ {\top} \cdot \prod_ {r = 2} ^ {l - 1} \left[ V _ {r} U _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] \right\| _ {F}.
$$

The Frobenius norm is invariant to multiplication by orthogonal matrices. Thus, we may multiply by  $V_{l}U_{l}^{\top}$  from the left:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| W _ {l} - W _ {l} ^ {\prime} \| _ {F} = \left\| V _ {l} \Sigma_ {l} V _ {l} ^ {\top} - \prod_ {2} ^ {r = l - 1} \left[ U _ {r} V _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] \cdot U _ {1} \Sigma_ {1} U _ {1} ^ {\top} \cdot \prod_ {r = 2} ^ {l - 1} \left[ V _ {r} U _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] \right\| _ {F} \\ = \left\| \sqrt {W _ {l} ^ {\top} W _ {l}} - \sqrt {W _ {l - 1} ^ {\prime} W _ {l - 1} ^ {\prime \top}} \right\| _ {F} \tag {55} \\ \leq \left\| \sqrt {W _ {l} ^ {\top} W _ {l}} - \sqrt {W _ {l - 1} W _ {l - 1} ^ {\top}} \right\| _ {F} + \left\| \sqrt {W _ {l - 1} W _ {l - 1} ^ {\top}} - \sqrt {W _ {l - 1} ^ {\prime} W _ {l - 1} ^ {\prime \top}} \right\| _ {F}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Since the unbalancedness magnitude of  $W_{1}, W_{2}, \ldots, W_{L}$  is  $\epsilon$ , from the Powers-Størmer inequality (Lemma 4.1 in [69]) we know that:

$$
\left\| \sqrt {W _ {l} ^ {\top} W _ {l}} - \sqrt {W _ {l - 1} W _ {l - 1} ^ {\top}} \right\| _ {F} \leq \sqrt {\left\| W _ {l} ^ {\top} W _ {l} - W _ {l - 1} W _ {l - 1} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {n u c l e a r}} \leq \sqrt {\epsilon}.
$$

Additionally, multiplying by  $U_{l-1} V_{l-1}^{\top}$  from the right:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \sqrt {W _ {l - 1} W _ {l - 1} ^ {\top}} - \sqrt {W _ {l - 1} ^ {\prime} W _ {l - 1} ^ {\prime \top}} \right\| _ {F} = \left\| U _ {l - 1} \Sigma_ {l - 1} U _ {l - 1} ^ {\top} - \prod_ {2} ^ {r = l - 1} \left[ U _ {r} V _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] U _ {1} \Sigma_ {1} U _ {1} ^ {\top} \prod_ {r = 2} ^ {l - 1} \left[ V _ {r} U _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] \right\| _ {F} \\ = \left\| U _ {l - 1} \Sigma_ {l - 1} V _ {l - 1} ^ {\top} - \prod_ {2} ^ {r = l - 1} \left[ U _ {r} V _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] U _ {1} \Sigma_ {1} U _ {1} ^ {\top} \prod_ {r = 2} ^ {l - 2} \left[ V _ {r} U _ {r} ^ {\top} \right] \right\| _ {F} \\ = \left\| W _ {l - 1} - W _ {l - 1} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F}. \\ \leq (l - 2) \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality is by the inductive assumption. Going back to Equation (55), we conclude:

$$
\left\| W _ {l} - W _ {l} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} \leq \sqrt {\epsilon} + (l - 2) \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} = (l - 1) \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}.
$$

# D.10 Proof of Lemma 2

Define  $g:[0,T]\to \mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}$  by  $g(t)\coloneqq \| \theta (t) - \theta^{\prime}(t)\|_{2}^{2}$ . For any  $t\in [0,T]$  it holds that:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \dot {g} (t) = 2 \left\langle \theta (t) - \theta^ {\prime} (t), \dot {\theta} (t) - \dot {\theta} ^ {\prime} (t) \right\rangle \\ = - 2 \left\langle \theta (t) - \theta^ {\prime} (t), \nabla f (\theta (t)) - \nabla f \left(\theta^ {\prime} (t)\right) \right\rangle . \\ \end{array}
$$

By the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and smoothness of  $f(\cdot)$ , we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \dot {g} (t) \leq 2 \| \theta (t) - \theta^ {\prime} (t) \| _ {2} \cdot \| \nabla f (\theta (t)) - \nabla f (\theta^ {\prime} (t)) \| _ {2} \\ \leq 2 \beta \| \theta (t) - \theta^ {\prime} (t) \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = 2 \beta \cdot g (t). \\ \end{array}
$$

Let  $\bar{t} \in [0, T]$ , and suppose that there exists  $t_0 \in [0, \bar{t}]$  for which  $g(t_0) = 0$ . Consider the initial value problem induced by gradient flow over  $f(\cdot)$  starting from the point  $\theta(t_0) = \theta'(t_0)$ . Since its solution on the interval  $[t_0, \bar{t}]$  is unique (by the definition of  $\theta(\cdot)$ ,  $\theta'(\cdot)$ ) there exist a solution lying within  $\mathcal{D}$ , and it not being unique would contradict, e.g., Theorem 2.2 in [81]), it holds that  $\theta(t) = \theta'(t)$  for any  $t \in [t_0, \bar{t}]$ . That is, Equation (19) trivially holds. Now assume that  $\forall t \in [0, \bar{t}]: g(t) > 0$ . Then:

$$
\frac {\dot {g} (t)}{g (t)} \leq 2 \beta \Rightarrow \int_ {t = 0} ^ {\bar {t}} \frac {\dot {g} (t)}{g (t)} d t \leq 2 \beta \bar {t} \Rightarrow \ln (g (\bar {t})) - \ln (g (0)) \leq 2 \beta \bar {t} \Rightarrow g (\bar {t}) \leq g (0) \cdot \exp (2 \beta \bar {t}).
$$

Taking the square root of both sides in the latter inequality concludes the proof.

# D.11 Proof of Proposition 5

We note that the following proof does not depend on the dimensions of the deep matrix factorization  $(d_0, d_1, \ldots, d_L$ ; see Section 3). That is, Proposition 5 holds for arbitrary dimensions, and is not limited to the square case where  $d_0 = d_1 = \ldots = d_L$ .

Let  $R' > 0$ , and define  $\mathcal{D}_{R'} := \{(W_1, W_2, \ldots, W_L) : \| (W_1, W_2, \ldots, W_L) \|_F < R'\}$ . For any  $(W_1, W_2, \ldots, W_L)$  and  $(W_1', W_2', \ldots, W_L')$  in  $\mathcal{D}_{R'}$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \nabla \phi \left(W _ {1}, W _ {2}, \dots , W _ {L}\right) - \nabla \phi \left(W _ {1} ^ {\prime}, W _ {2} ^ {\prime}, \dots , W _ {L} ^ {\prime}\right) \right\| _ {F} \\ = \sqrt {\sum_ {l = 1} ^ {L} \left\| \prod_ {r = l + 1} ^ {L} W _ {r} ^ {\top} \cdot \nabla \ell \left(W _ {L : 1}\right) \cdot \prod_ {r = 1} ^ {l - 1} W _ {r} ^ {\top} - \prod_ {r = l + 1} ^ {L} W _ {r} ^ {\prime \top} \cdot \nabla \ell \left(W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime}\right) \cdot \prod_ {r = 1} ^ {l - 1} W _ {r} ^ {\prime \top} \right\| _ {F} ^ {2}}. \tag {56} \\ \end{array}
$$

Since  $(\nabla \ell(W_{L:1}))_{i,j} = (W_{L:1})_{i,j} - b_{i,j}$  if  $(i,j) \in \Omega$ , and otherwise  $(\nabla \ell(W_{L:1}))_{i,j} = 0$ , we have that  $\| \nabla \ell(W_{L:1}) - \nabla \ell(W_{L:1}') \|_F \leq \| W_{L:1} - W_{L:1}' \|_F$  and  $\| \nabla \ell(W_{L:1}) \|_F \leq \| W_{L:1} \|_F + B$ . For each  $l \in [L]$ , Lemma 12 and sub-multiplicativity of the Frobenius norm then yield:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \prod_ {r = l + 1} ^ {L} W _ {r} ^ {\top} \cdot \nabla \ell \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) \cdot \prod_ {r = 1} ^ {l - 1} W _ {r} ^ {\top} - \prod_ {r = l + 1} ^ {L} W _ {r} ^ {\prime \top} \cdot \nabla \ell \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) \cdot \prod_ {r = 1} ^ {l - 1} W _ {r} ^ {\prime \top} \right\| _ {F} \\ \leq R ^ {\prime L - 2} \left(R ^ {\prime L} + B\right) \cdot \sum_ {r = 1} ^ {L} \left\| W _ {r} - W _ {r} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} + R ^ {\prime L - 1} \cdot \left\| \nabla \ell \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) - \nabla \ell \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) \right\| _ {F} \\ \leq R ^ {\prime L - 2} \left(R ^ {\prime L} + B\right) \cdot \sum_ {r = 1} ^ {L} \left\| W _ {r} - W _ {r} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} + R ^ {\prime L - 1} \left\| W _ {L: 1} - W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} \\ \leq R ^ {\prime L - 2} \left(R ^ {\prime L} + B\right) \cdot \sum_ {r = 1} ^ {L} \left\| W _ {r} - W _ {r} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} + R ^ {\prime 2 L - 2} \cdot \sum_ {r = 1} ^ {L} \left\| W _ {r} - W _ {r} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} \\ = \left(2 R ^ {\prime 2 L - 2} + B R ^ {\prime L - 2}\right) \cdot \sum_ {r = 1} ^ {L} \| W _ {r} - W _ {r} ^ {\prime} \| _ {F}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Plugging the inequality above into Equation (56), we conclude:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \nabla \phi \left(W _ {1}, W _ {2}, \dots , W _ {L}\right) - \nabla \phi \left(W _ {1} ^ {\prime}, W _ {2} ^ {\prime}, \dots , W _ {L} ^ {\prime}\right) \right\| _ {F} \\ \leq \sqrt {L \cdot \left(2 R ^ {\prime 2 L - 2} + B R ^ {\prime L - 2}\right) ^ {2} \cdot \left(\sum_ {l = 1} ^ {L} \| W _ {l} - W _ {l} ^ {\prime} \| _ {F}\right) ^ {2}} \\ \leq \sqrt {L ^ {2} \cdot \left(2 R ^ {\prime 2 L - 2} + B R ^ {\prime L - 2}\right) ^ {2} \cdot \sum_ {l = 1} ^ {L} \left\| W _ {l} - W _ {l} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} ^ {2}} \\ = L R ^ {\prime L - 2} \left(2 R ^ {\prime L} + B\right) \cdot \left\| \left(W _ {1}, W _ {2}, \dots , W _ {L}\right) - \left(W _ {1} ^ {\prime}, W _ {2} ^ {\prime}, \dots , W _ {L} ^ {\prime}\right) \right\| _ {F}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second inequality is by  $\sum_{l=1}^{L}\|W_l-W_l'\|_F\leq(L\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{L}\|W_l-W_l'\|_F^2)^{0.5}$ . That is, the objective  $\phi(\cdot)$  is  $LR'^{L-2}(2R'^L+B)$ -smooth over  $\mathcal{D}_{R'}$ . For any  $\bar{t}\in[0,T]$ , from Lemma 2 we have that:

$$
\left\| \theta (\bar {t}) - \theta^ {\prime} (\bar {t}) \right\| _ {F} \leq \left\| \theta (0) - \theta^ {\prime} (0) \right\| _ {F} \cdot \exp \left(L R ^ {\prime L - 2} \left(2 R ^ {\prime L} + B\right) \cdot \bar {t}\right).
$$

Notice that  $R$  is finite (it is the supremum of a continuous function over a closed domain). Since the inequality above holds for all  $R' > R$ , taking the limit  $R' \to R^+$  yields Equation (20).

# D.12 Proof of Lemma 3

For any  $l \in [L]$ :

$$
\dot {W} _ {l} (t) = - \prod_ {r = l + 1} ^ {L} W _ {r} (t) ^ {\top} \cdot \nabla \ell \left(W _ {L: 1} (t)\right) \cdot \prod_ {r = 1} ^ {l - 1} W _ {r} (t) ^ {\top}, \forall t \geq 0.
$$

This implies that for any  $l \in [L - 1]$ :

$$
\dot {W} _ {l} (t) W _ {l} (t) ^ {\top} = W _ {l + 1} (t) ^ {\top} \dot {W} _ {l + 1} (t), \forall t \geq 0.
$$

Adding the transpose of the latter equality to itself, we have:

$$
\frac {d}{d t} \left(W _ {l} (t) W _ {l} (t) ^ {\top}\right) = \frac {d}{d t} \left(W _ {l + 1} (t) ^ {\top} W _ {l + 1} (t)\right), \forall t \geq 0.
$$

Hence,  $W_{l + 1}(t)^{\top}W_{l + 1}(t) - W_{l}(t)W_{l}(t)^{\top} = W_{l + 1}(0)^{\top}W_{l + 1}(0) - W_{l}(0)W_{l}(0)^{\top}$  for all  $t \geq 0$ . Taking the nuclear norm of both sides and maximizing over  $l \in [L - 1]$  yields the desired result.

# D.13 Proof of Theorem 3

The proof of Theorem 1 (Subappendix D.5) relies solely on the fact that  $\operatorname{det}(W_{L:1}(t))$  remains positive through time. In particular, it establishes that the bounds on (quasi-)norms, effective rank and distance from infimal rank (Equations (8), (9) and (10) respectively) are guaranteed to hold for any  $t \geq 0$  for which  $\operatorname{det}(W_{L:1}(t)) > 0$ . Therefore, if  $\operatorname{det}(W_{L:1}(t)) > 0$  for all  $t \geq 0$ , the proof concludes. Otherwise, let  $T \in [0,\infty)$  be the initial time for which  $\operatorname{det}(W_{L:1}(T)) = 0$ . Formally, define:

$$
T := \inf  \left\{t \mid t \in [ 0, \infty) \text {a n d} \det  \left(W _ {L:} (t)\right) = 0 \right\}.
$$

Since  $\operatorname{det}(W_{L:1}(t))$  is continuous in  $t$ , the set on the right-hand side is non-empty, closed, and bounded from below. Thus,  $T$  is well defined (i.e.  $-\infty < T < \infty$ ),  $\operatorname{det}(W_{L:1}(T)) = 0$ , and  $\operatorname{det}(W_{L:1}(t)) > 0$  for any  $t \in [0,T)$  (recall that by assumption the determinant is positive at initialization). That is, the results of Theorem 1 hold over  $[0,T)$ . Let:

$$
R := \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} {\left[ \frac {\left(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}\right) ^ {4}}{2 ^ {1 6}} \cdot \ln \left(\frac {1}{\epsilon}\right) \right] ^ {1 / 6} - 1} & , \text {i f d e p t h L = 2} \\ {\left[ \frac {\left(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}\right) ^ {4}}{2 ^ {2 L + 8} L ^ {4}} \cdot \frac {1}{\epsilon^ {1 / 3 2}} \right] ^ {1 / (4 L - 2)} - 1} & , \text {i f d e p t h L \geq 3} \end{array} \right. \tag {57}
$$

The proof proceeds in two parts. First, assuming that  $\max_{l\in [L]}\| W_l(t)\| _F\leq R$  for any  $t\in [0,T]$ , Subappendix D.13.1 establishes Equation (21) by deriving a lower bound on  $T$ . Otherwise, if there exists  $t\in [0,T]$  with  $\max_{l\in [L]}\| W_l(t)\| _F > R$ , Subappendix D.13.2 shows that Equations (22), (23) and (24) jointly hold for some  $\bar{t}\in [0,t]$ , completing the proof.

# D.13.1 Proof of Equation (21) (if weight matrices are bounded by  $R$ )

Assume that  $\max_{l\in [L]}\| W_l(t)\| _F\leq R$  for any  $t\in [0,T]$ . The loss during gradient flow is monotonically non-increasing (Lemma 17). This implies that  $\ell (W_{L:1}(t))\leq \ell_{init}$  for all  $t\geq 0$ . Hence,  $\min \{((W_{L:1}(t))_{1,2} - 1)^2,((W_{L:1}(t))_{2,1} - 1)^2\} \leq \ell_{init}$  and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1} (t)\right) \geq \frac {1}{\sqrt {2}} \left\| W _ {L: 1} (t) \right\| _ {F} \\ \geq \frac {1}{\sqrt {2}} \max  \left\{\left(W _ {L: 1} (t)\right) _ {1, 2}, \left(W _ {L: 1} (t)\right) _ {2, 1} \right\} \tag {58} \\ \geq \frac {1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}}{\sqrt {2}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

for all  $t\geq 0$  . Define:

$$
t _ {0} := \left\{ \begin{array}{c} 0 \\ \sup  \left\{t | t \in [ 0, T ] \text {a n d} \sigma_ {2} (W _ {L: 1} (t)) = \frac {1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}}{2} \right\}, \text {o t h e r w i s e} \end{array} \right.   , \forall t \in [ 0, T ]: \sigma_ {2} (W _ {L: 1} (t)) <   \frac {1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}}{2}
$$

By Lemma 5,  $\sigma_{2}(W_{L:1}(t))$  is a continuous function of  $t$ . Combined with the fact that  $\sigma_{2}(W_{L:1}(T)) = 0$  (recall the determinant is zero at  $T$ ), we have that the set in the alternative case above is non-empty and compact. Thus,  $t_0$  is well defined (i.e.  $-\infty < t_0 < \infty$ ). If  $t_0 = 0$ , then  $\sigma_{2}(W_{L:1}(t_0)) > 0$  since by assumption  $\operatorname*{det}(W_{L:1}(0)) > 0$ . Otherwise, continuity of  $\sigma_{2}(W_{L:1}(t))$  with respect to  $t$  implies that  $\sigma_{2}(W_{L:1}(t_0)) = (1 - \sqrt{\ell_{init}}) / 2 > 0$ . Therefore, in either case  $t_0 < T$ , and  $[t_0, T]$  is the interval preceding  $T$  over which  $\sigma_{2}(W_{L:1}(t)) \leq (1 - \sqrt{\ell_{init}}) / 2$ .

Fix an arbitrary  $\hat{t} \in [t_0, T]$ . For conciseness, we hereinafter omit the time index in functions of  $t$  at  $\hat{t}$ , e.g.  $W_{L:1}$  will be shorthand for  $W_{L:1}(\hat{t})$ . We now seek to derive a lower bound on  $\frac{d}{dt} \sigma_2^2(W_{L:1})$ . Integrating said bound will yield the desired result. By Lemmas 3 and 1, there exist balanced matrices  $W_1', W_2', \ldots, W_L'$  satisfying:

$$
\left\| W _ {l} - W _ {l} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} \leq (l - 1) \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}, \forall l \in [ L ]. \tag {59}
$$

Applying Lemma 12, and noticing that  $\max_{l\in [L]}\| W_l'\| _F\leq R + (L - 1)\cdot \sqrt{\epsilon}\leq R + 1$ , we bound the distance between the induced product matrices:

$$
\left\| W _ {L: 1} - W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} \leq (R + 1) ^ {L - 1} \cdot \sum_ {l = 1} ^ {L} (l - 1) \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} \leq \frac {1}{2} L ^ {2} (R + 1) ^ {L - 1} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}. \tag {60}
$$

Consider a gradient flow path originating from  $W_1', W_2', \ldots, W_L'$ , where for simplicity we regard the initial time of this path as  $\hat{t}$ . For a balanced flow, Lemma 5 characterizes the movement of the product matrix's singular values. Omitting the time index, by the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and the fact that the Frobenius norm of an outer product between unit vectors is 1, we have that:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {d}{d t} \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} \right| = \left| 2 \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) \cdot \frac {d}{d t} \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) \right| \\ \leq 2 L \cdot \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {3 - 2 / L} \cdot \| \nabla \ell \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) \| _ {F} \\ \leq 4 L (R + 1) ^ {L} \cdot \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {3 - 2 / L}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last transition is by  $\| \nabla \ell (W_{L:1}^{\prime})\|_{F}\leq \| W_{L:1}^{\prime}\|_{F} + \sqrt{2}\leq 2(R + 1)^{L}$ . Applying the singular values perturbation bound  $\sigma_2(W_{L:1}^{\prime})\leq \sigma_2(W_{L:1}) + \| W_{L:1} - W_{L:1}^{\prime}\|_{F}$  (Corollary 8.6.2 in [33]), the bound in Equation (60), and Jensen's inequality, we arrive at:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {d}{d t} \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} \right| \leq 4 L (R + 1) ^ {L} \cdot \left[ \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) + \frac {1}{2} L ^ {2} (R + 1) ^ {L - 1} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} \right] ^ {3 - 2 / L} \\ \leq 4 \cdot 2 ^ {2 - 2 / L} L (R + 1) ^ {L} \cdot \left[ \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {3 - 2 / L} + \left(\frac {1}{2} L ^ {2} (R + 1) ^ {L - 1} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}\right) ^ {3 - 2 / L} \right] \\ \leq 2 ^ {4} L (R + 1) ^ {L} \cdot \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {3 - 2 / L} + 2 \cdot L ^ {7 - 4 / L} (R + 1) ^ {4 L - 5 + 2 / L} \cdot \epsilon^ {3 / 2 - 1 / L}. \tag {61} \\ \end{array}
$$

We turn our attention to  $\left| \frac{d}{dt} \sigma_2(W_{L:1})^2 - \frac{d}{dt} \sigma_2(W_{L:1}')^2 \right|$ . Recalling that  $W_{L:1}$  is a 2-by-2 matrix, its minimal squared singular value can be written as:

$$
\sigma_ {2} (W _ {L: 1}) ^ {2} = \frac {1}{2} \left(\| W _ {L: 1} \| _ {F} ^ {2} - \sqrt {\| W _ {L : 1} \| _ {F} ^ {4} - 4 \det (W _ {L : 1}) ^ {2}}\right).
$$

Differentiating with respect to time, while noticing that  $(\| W_{L:1}\|_F^4 - 4\operatorname{det}(W_{L:1})^2)^{0.5} = \sigma_1(W_{L:1})^2 - \sigma_2(W_{L:1})^2$ , we obtain:

$$
\frac {d}{d t} \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} = \left\langle W _ {L: 1}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\rangle - \frac {\| W _ {L : 1} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \left\langle W _ {L : 1} , \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L : 1} \right\rangle + 2 \det  \left(W _ {L : 1}\right) \left\langle A _ {W _ {L : 1}} , \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L : 1} \right\rangle}{\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L : 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L : 1}\right) ^ {2}},
$$

with  $A_{W_{L:1}} \coloneqq \left( \begin{array}{cc} (W_{L:1})_{2,2} & -(W_{L:1})_{2,1} \\ -(W_{L:1})_{1,2} & (W_{L:1})_{1,1} \end{array} \right)$ . The same derivation holds for  $\frac{d}{dt}\sigma_2(W_{L:1}')$ , with  $W_{L:1}'$  in place of  $W_{L:1}$ . Applying the triangle inequality,  $\left| \frac{d}{dt}\sigma_2(W_{L:1})^2 - \frac{d}{dt}\sigma_2(W_{L:1}')^2 \right|$  is upper bounded by the sum of the expressions in Equations (62), (63) and (64) below:

$$
\left| \left\langle W _ {L: 1}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\rangle - \left\langle W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\rangle \right|, \tag {62}
$$

$$
\left| \frac {\left\| W _ {L : 1} \right\| _ {F} ^ {2} \left\langle W _ {L : 1} , \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L : 1} \right\rangle}{\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L : 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L : 1}\right) ^ {2}} - \frac {\left\| W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} ^ {2} \left\langle W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime} , \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime} \right\rangle}{\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}} \right|, \tag {63}
$$

$$
\left. \right.\left| \frac {2 \det  \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)\left\langle A _ {W _ {L : 1}} , \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L : 1} \right\rangle}{\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L : 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L : 1}\right) ^ {2}} - \frac {2 \det  \left(W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime}\right)\left\langle A _ {W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime}} , \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime} \right\rangle}{\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}} \right| . \tag {64}
$$

Lemmas 28, 29 and 30 (with the aid of Lemma (27)) derive upper bounds for the expressions in Equations (62), (63) and (64), respectively. Then, putting it all together, we arrive at:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {d}{d t} \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \frac {d}{d t} \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} \right| \leq 6 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {4 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} + \frac {3 8 4 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {8 L - 3}}{\left(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}\right) ^ {4}} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} \\ + \frac {7 6 8 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {8 L - 3}}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {4}} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} \\ \leq \frac {1 1 5 8 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {8 L - 3}}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {4}} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Going back to Equation (61), this implies that:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {d}{d t} \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} \right| \leq \left| \frac {d}{d t} \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} \right| + \left| \frac {d}{d t} \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \frac {d}{d t} \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} \right| \\ \leq 2 ^ {4} L (R + 1) ^ {L} \cdot \left(\sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2}\right) ^ {3 / 2 - 1 / L} + \frac {2 ^ {1 1} L ^ {7} \left(R + 1\right) ^ {8 L - 3}}{\left(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {\text {i n i t}}}\right) ^ {4}} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}. \\ \end{array}
$$

We are now in a position to achieve the sought-after lower bound on  $T$ . If  $L = 2$ , according to Lemma 16:

$$
\sigma_ {2} (W _ {L: 1} (T)) ^ {2} \geq \left(\frac {2 ^ {7} L ^ {6} (R + 1) ^ {7 L - 3}}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {4}} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} + \sigma_ {i n i t} ^ {2}\right) \cdot e ^ {- 2 ^ {4} L (R + 1) ^ {L} (T - t _ {0})} - \frac {2 ^ {7} L ^ {6} (R + 1) ^ {7 L - 3}}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {4}} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon},
$$

where  $\sigma_{init} := \min \{\sigma_2(W_{L:1}(0)), (1 - \sqrt{\ell_{init}}) / 2\} = \sigma_2(W_{L:1}(t_0))$ . Due to the fact that  $T$  is the initial point in time for which  $\operatorname{det}(W_{L:1}(T)) = 0$ , the right hand side must be non-positive, in which case:

$$
T \geq \frac {1}{2 ^ {4} L (R + 1) ^ {L}} \left[ \frac {1}{2} \ln \left(\frac {1}{\epsilon}\right) - \ln \left(\frac {2 ^ {7} L ^ {6} (R + 1) ^ {7 L - 3}}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {4} \sigma_ {i n i t} ^ {2}}\right) \right].
$$

Since  $\ln \left(2^{7}L^{6}(R + 1)^{7L - 3} / (1 - \sqrt{\ell_{init}})^{4}\sigma_{init}^2\right)\cdot 2^{-4}L^{-1}(R + 1)^{-L}\leq \ln \left(e / (1 - \sqrt{\ell_{init}})\sigma_{init}\right)$ , plugging in the value of  $R$  (Equation (57)) leads to the desired result (case  $L = 2$  in Equation (21)).

Similarly, for depth  $L \geq 3$ , Lemma 16 gives the following lower bound:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {2} (W _ {L: 1} (T)) ^ {2} \\ \geq \frac {1}{b _ {L , R} ^ {\frac {2 L}{3 L - 2}} \left[ b _ {L , R} ^ {\frac {2 L}{3 L - 2}} (1 / 2 - 1 / L) (T - t _ {0}) + \left(a _ {L , R} ^ {\frac {2 L}{3 L - 2}} + b _ {L , R} ^ {\frac {2 L}{3 L - 2}} \cdot \sigma_ {i n i t} ^ {2}\right) ^ {\frac {2 - L}{2 L}} \right] ^ {\frac {2 L}{L - 2}}} - \left(\frac {a _ {L , R}}{b _ {L , R}}\right) ^ {\frac {2 L}{3 L - 2}} \\ \geq \frac {1}{b _ {L , R} ^ {\frac {2 L}{3 L - 2}} \left[ b _ {L , R} ^ {\frac {2 L}{3 L - 2}} \cdot T + b _ {L , R} ^ {\frac {2 - L}{3 L - 2}} \cdot \sigma_ {i n i t} ^ {\frac {2 - L}{L}} \right] ^ {\frac {2 L}{L - 2}}} - \left(\frac {a _ {L , R}}{b _ {L , R}}\right) ^ {\frac {2 L}{3 L - 2}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $a_{L,R} \coloneqq \frac{2^{11}L^7(R + 1)^{8L - 3}}{(1 - \sqrt{\ell_{init}})^4} \cdot \sqrt{\epsilon}$  and  $b_{L,R} \coloneqq 2^4 L(R + 1)^L$ . Since  $\operatorname*{det}(W_{L:1}(T)) = 0$ , the lower bound must be non-positive, in which case:

$$
T \geq b _ {L, R} ^ {- \frac {2 L}{3 L - 2}} \cdot a _ {L, R} ^ {- \frac {L - 2}{3 L - 2}} - b _ {L, R} ^ {- 1} \cdot \sigma_ {i n i t} ^ {- \frac {L - 2}{L}}.
$$

Noticing that  $b_{L,R}^{-1} \leq 2^{-(5L + 5)}$ , and replacing  $a_{L,R}, b_{L,R}$  with their explicit expressions, we arrive at:

$$
T \geq 2 ^ {\frac {- 1 9 L + 2 2}{3 L - 2}} (1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {\frac {4 L - 8}{3 L - 2}} L ^ {\frac {- 9 L + 1 4}{3 L - 2}} (R + 1) ^ {- \frac {- 1 0 L ^ {2} + 1 9 L - 6}{3 L - 2}} \cdot \epsilon^ {- \frac {L - 2}{6 L - 4}} - 2 ^ {- (5 L + 5)} \sigma_ {i n i t} ^ {- \frac {L - 2}{L}}.
$$

To simplify the result, we may lower bound the exponent of  $R$  by  $-4L$ . Then, plugging in the value of  $R$  (Equation (57)), and applying straightforward bounds to the exponents of 2,  $(1 - \sqrt{\ell_{init}}), L$  and  $\epsilon$ , concludes this part of the proof (i.e. establishes the case  $L \geq 3$  in Equation (21)):

$$
\begin{array}{l} T \geq 2 ^ {\frac {4 L ^ {2} + 1 6 L}{2 L - 1} - \frac {1 9 L - 2 2}{3 L - 2}} (1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {\frac {4 L - 8}{3 L - 2} - \frac {1 6 L}{4 L - 2}} L ^ {\frac {1 6 L}{4 L - 2} - \frac {9 L - 1 4}{3 L - 2}} \cdot \epsilon^ {\frac {L}{8 (4 L - 2)} - \frac {L - 2}{6 L - 4}} - 2 ^ {- (5 L + 5)} \sigma_ {i n i t} ^ {- \frac {L - 2}{L}} \\ \geq 2 ^ {4 L / 3} (1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {- 2} L \cdot \epsilon^ {- \frac {3 L - 8}{3 2 L - 1 6}} - 2 ^ {- (5 L + 5)} \sigma_ {i n i t} ^ {- \frac {L - 2}{L}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/734d483512d9c775b72d4ed6f156029f79bce93b1df6557d6bcafd9e67116d0d.jpg)

# D.13.1.1 Auxiliary Lemmas

Lemma 27. In the context of the proof for Equation (21) (Subappendix D.13.1), the following inequalities hold:

$$
\max  \left\{\left\| \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\| _ {F}, \left\| \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} \right\} \leq 2 L (R + 1) ^ {3 L - 2}, \tag {65}
$$

$$
\left\| \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} - \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} \leq 5 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {3 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}, \tag {66}
$$

$$
\left. \left| \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \left(\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}\right) \right| \leq 2 L ^ {2} (R + 1) ^ {2 L - 1} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}, \right. \tag {67}
$$

$$
\left(\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2}\right) \left(\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}\right) \geq (1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {\text {i n i t}}}) ^ {4} / 3 2. \tag {68}
$$

Proof. Starting with Equation (65), the derivative of  $W_{L:1}$  with respect to time is:

$$
\frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} = - \sum_ {l = 1} ^ {L} \prod_ {l + 1} ^ {r = L} W _ {r} \prod_ {r = l + 1} ^ {L} W _ {r} ^ {\top} \cdot \nabla \ell \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) \cdot \prod_ {r = 1} ^ {l - 1} W _ {r} ^ {\top} \prod_ {1} ^ {r = l - 1} W _ {r}.
$$

Therefore:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\| _ {F} \leq \sum_ {l = 1} ^ {L} \left\| \prod_ {l + 1} ^ {r = L} W _ {r} \prod_ {r = l + 1} ^ {L} W _ {r} ^ {\top} \cdot \nabla \ell \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) \cdot \prod_ {r = 1} ^ {l - 1} W _ {r} ^ {\top} \prod_ {1} ^ {r = l - 1} W _ {r} \right\| _ {F} \\ \leq 2 L (R + 1) ^ {3 L - 2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last transition is by submultiplicativity of the Frobenius norm, the fact that  $\| W_r\| _F\leq R + 1$  for  $r\in [L]$ , and  $\| \nabla \ell (W_{L:1})\| _F\leq 2(R + 1)^L$ . The exact same bound can be derived for  $\left\| \frac{d}{dt} W_{L:1}'\right\| _F$ , completing the proof of Equation (65).

Moving on to Equation (66):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} - \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} \leq \sum_ {l = 1} ^ {L} \left\| \prod_ {l + 1} ^ {r = L} W _ {r} \prod_ {r = l + 1} ^ {L} W _ {r} ^ {\top} \cdot \nabla \ell \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) \cdot \prod_ {r = 1} ^ {l - 1} W _ {r} ^ {\top} \right. \prod_ {1} ^ {r = l - 1} W _ {r} \\ \left. - \prod_ {l + 1} ^ {r = L} W _ {r} ^ {\prime} \prod_ {r = l + 1} ^ {L} W _ {r} ^ {\prime \top} \cdot \nabla \ell \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) \cdot \prod_ {r = 1} ^ {l - 1} W _ {r} ^ {\prime \top} \prod_ {1} ^ {r = l - 1} W _ {r} ^ {\prime} \right| _ {F}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Noticing that  $\| \nabla \ell (W_{L:1}) - \nabla \ell (W_{L:1}^{\prime})\|_{F}\leq \| W_{L:1} - W_{L:1}^{\prime}\|_{F}$ , according to Lemma 12 we may bound each term in the sum by  $4(R + 1)^{3L - 3}\cdot \sum_{l = 1}^{L}\| W_{l} - W_{l}^{\prime}\|_{F} + (R + 1)^{2L - 2}\cdot \| W_{L:1} - W_{L:1}^{\prime}\|_{F}$ . Applying the bounds from Equations (59) and (60) then establishes Equation (66):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} - \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} \leq L \cdot \left[ 4 L ^ {2} (R + 1) ^ {3 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} + \frac {1}{2} L ^ {2} (R + 1) ^ {3 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} \right] \\ \leq 5 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {3 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Next, Equation (67) is straightforwardly derived using a perturbation bound on the singular values (Corollary 8.6.2 in [33]):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} \right| = \left| \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) - \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) \right| \cdot \left| \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) + \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) \right| \\ \leq 2 (R + 1) ^ {L} \left\| W _ {L: 1} - W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} \\ \leq L ^ {2} (R + 1) ^ {2 L - 1} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last transition is by Equation (60). The same derivation shows that a similar inequality holds for  $\sigma_{2}(\cdot)$ , establishing Equation (67):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \left(\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}\right) \right| \\ \leq \left| \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} \right| + \left| \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} \right| \\ \leq 2 L ^ {2} (R + 1) ^ {2 L - 1} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Lastly, recall that  $\sigma_{2}(W_{L:1}(t))\leq (1 - \sqrt{\ell_{init}}) / 2$  over  $[t_0,T]$ . Combined with Equation (58), this implies that  $\sigma_{1}(W_{L:1})^{2} - \sigma_{2}(W_{L:1})^{2}\geq (1 - \sqrt{\ell_{init}})^{2} / 4$ . Then, by Equation (67) we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} \\ \geq \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \left| \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \left(\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}\right) \right| \\ \geq (1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {2} / 4 - 2 L ^ {2} (R + 1) ^ {2 L - 1} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Noticing that  $\epsilon \leq \frac{(1 - \sqrt{\ell_{init}})^4}{2^{2L + 8}L^4(R + 1)^{4L - 2}}$ , in which case  $2L^2 (R + 1)^{2L - 1}\cdot \sqrt{\epsilon}\leq (1 - \sqrt{\ell_{init}})^2 /8$  concludes the proof:

$$
\left(\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2}\right) \left(\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}\right) \geq \frac {\left(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}\right) ^ {2}}{4} \cdot \frac {\left(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}\right) ^ {2}}{8}.
$$

![](images/05fe0eb5300975c62642e7df590db8f49d2ee3e81e9ad185052e52335c39d857.jpg)

Lemma 28. In the context of the proof for Equation (21) (Subappendix D.13.1):

$$
\left| \left\langle W _ {L: 1}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\rangle - \left\langle W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\rangle \right| \leq 6 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {4 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon},
$$

and

$$
\left| \left\langle A _ {W _ {L: 1}}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\rangle - \left\langle A _ {W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\rangle \right| \leq 6 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {4 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}.
$$

Proof. Starting with the former inequality, adding and subtracting  $\left\langle W_{L:1}^{\prime}, \frac{d}{dt} W_{L:1} \right\rangle$ , followed by the triangle and Cauchy-Schwartz inequalities, we have that  $\left| \left\langle W_{L:1}, \frac{d}{dt} W_{L:1} \right\rangle - \left\langle W_{L:1}^{\prime}, \frac{d}{dt} W_{L:1}^{\prime} \right\rangle \right|$  is bounded by:

$$
\left\| W _ {L: 1} - W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} \cdot \left\| \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\| _ {F} + \left\| W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} \cdot \left\| \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} - \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F}.
$$

From Equation (60) we know that  $\| W_{L:1} - W_{L:1}'\|_F \leq (1/2)L^2(R + 1)^{L-1} \cdot \sqrt{\epsilon}$ . Additionally, by sub-multiplicativity of the Frobenius norm,  $\| W_{L:1}'\|_F \leq (R + 1)^L$ . Applying Equations (65) and (66) from Lemma 27, we conclude:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \left\langle W _ {L: 1}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\rangle - \left\langle W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\rangle \right| \leq L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {4 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} + 5 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {4 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} \\ = 6 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {4 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}. \\ \end{array}
$$

For  $\left|\left\langle A_{W_{L:1}}, \frac{d}{dt} W_{L:1}\right\rangle - \left\langle A_{W_{L:1}', \frac{d}{dt} W_{L:1}'}\right\rangle \right|$ , similar proof steps establish the same upper bound since  $\left\| A_{W_{L:1}} - A_{W_{L:1}'}\right\|_F = \| W_{L:1} - W_{L:1}'\|_F$  and  $\left\| A_{W_{L:1}'}\right\|_F = \| W_{L:1}'\|_F$ .

Lemma 29. In the context of the proof for Equation (21) (Subappendix D.13.1):

$$
\left| \frac {\| W _ {L : 1} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \left\langle W _ {L : 1} , \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L : 1} \right\rangle}{\sigma_ {1} (W _ {L : 1}) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} (W _ {L : 1}) ^ {2}} - \frac {\| W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \left\langle W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime} , \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime} \right\rangle}{\sigma_ {1} (W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} (W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}} \right| \leq \frac {3 8 4 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {8 L - 3}}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {4}} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}.
$$

Proof. By Equation (68), it suffices to show that:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {3 2}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {4}} \left| (\sigma_ {1} (W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} (W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}) \alpha_ {W _ {L: 1}} - (\sigma_ {1} (W _ {L: 1}) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} (W _ {L: 1}) ^ {2}) \alpha_ {W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}} \right| \\ \leq \frac {3 8 4 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {8 L - 3}}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {4}} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\alpha_{W_{L:1}} := \| W_{L:1} \|_F^2 \left\langle W_{L:1}, \frac{d}{dt} W_{L:1} \right\rangle$ , and  $\alpha_{W_{L:1}'}$  is defined similarly for  $W_{L:1}'$ . Focusing on the expression in absolute value, adding and subtracting  $(\sigma_1(W_{L:1})^2 - \sigma_2(W_{L:1})^2) \alpha_{W_{L:1}}$ , and applying the triangle inequality, leads to:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {3 2}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {4}} \left[ \left| \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} - \left(\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2}\right) \right| \cdot | \alpha_ {W _ {L: 1}} | \right. \tag {69} \\ \left. + \left| \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} \right| \cdot \left| \alpha_ {W _ {L: 1}} - \alpha_ {W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}} \right| \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

We consider each of these terms separately. From Equation (67) in Lemma 27 we know that  $\left|\sigma_{1}(W_{L:1}^{\prime})^{2} - \sigma_{2}(W_{L:1}^{\prime})^{2} - (\sigma_{1}(W_{L:1})^{2} - \sigma_{2}(W_{L:1})^{2}\right| \leq 2L^{2}(R + 1)^{2L - 1} \cdot \sqrt{\epsilon}$ . Equation (65) and the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality give:

$$
\left| \alpha_ {W _ {L: 1}} \right| \leq \left\| W _ {L: 1} \right\| _ {F} ^ {3} \cdot \left\| \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\| _ {F} \leq 2 L (R + 1) ^ {6 L - 2}.
$$

Additionally,  $\left|\sigma_{1}(W_{L:1})^{2} - \sigma_{2}(W_{L:1})^{2}\right| \leq \sigma_{1}(W_{L:1})^{2} + \sigma_{2}(W_{L:1})^{2} = \| W_{L:1}\|_{F}^{2} \leq (R + 1)^{2L}$ . By adding and subtracting  $\| W_{L:1}'\|_{F}^{2}\langle W_{L:1},\frac{d}{dt} W_{L:1}\rangle$ , we may upper bound  $\left|\alpha_{W_{L:1}} - \alpha_{W_{L:1}'}\right|$  by:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \| W _ {L: 1} \| _ {F} ^ {2} - \| W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \right| \left| \left\langle W _ {L: 1}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\rangle \right| + \| W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \left| \left\langle W _ {L: 1}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\rangle - \left\langle W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\rangle \right| \\ \leq \left| \| W _ {L: 1} \| _ {F} + \| W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \| _ {F} \right| \cdot \left| \| W _ {L: 1} \| _ {F} - \| W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \| _ {F} \right| \cdot \left\| W _ {L: 1} \right\| _ {F} \left\| \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\| _ {F} \\ + \| W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \left| \left\langle W _ {L: 1}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\rangle - \left\langle W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\rangle \right| \\ \leq 2 (R + 1) ^ {2 L} \left| \left\| W _ {L: 1} \right\| _ {F} - \left\| W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} \right| \cdot \left\| \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\| _ {F} \\ + (R + 1) ^ {2 L} \left| \left\langle W _ {L: 1}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\rangle - \left\langle W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\rangle \right| \\ \leq 2 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {6 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} + 6 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {6 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} \\ = 8 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {6 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}. \\ \end{array}
$$

where the third transition is due to Equations (60), (65), and Lemma 28. Putting it all together, the expression in Equation (69) is upper bounded by:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {3 2}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {4}} \left[ 4 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {8 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} \cdot + 8 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {8 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} \right] \\ = \frac {3 8 4 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {8 L - 3}}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {4}} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Lemma 30. In the context of the proof for Equation (21) (Subappendix D.13.1):

$$
\left| \frac {2 \det  \left(W _ {L : 1}\right) \left\langle A _ {W _ {L : 1}} , \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L : 1} \right\rangle}{\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L : 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L : 1}\right) ^ {2}} - \frac {2 \det  \left(W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime}\right) \left\langle A _ {W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime}} , \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime} \right\rangle}{\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L : 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}} \right| \leq \frac {7 6 8 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {8 L - 3}}{\left(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}\right) ^ {4}} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}.
$$

Proof. The proof follows a line similar to that of Lemma 29. Applying the bound from Equation (68) in Lemma 27, we arrive at the following upper bound for the left hand side above:

$$
\frac {6 4}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {4}} \left| (\sigma_ {1} (W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} (W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}) \beta_ {W _ {L: 1}} - (\sigma_ {1} (W _ {L: 1}) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} (W _ {L: 1}) ^ {2}) \beta_ {W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}} \right|,
$$

where  $\beta_{W_{L:1}}\coloneqq \operatorname *{det}(W_{L:1})\left\langle A_{W_{L:1}},\frac{d}{dt} W_{L:1}\right\rangle$  , and  $\beta_{W_{L:1}^{\prime}}$  is defined similarly for  $W_{L:1}^{\prime}$  . Focusing on the expression in absolute value, we add and subtract  $(\sigma_1(W_{L:1})^2 -\sigma_2(W_{L:1})^2)\beta_{W_{L:1}}$  and apply the triangle inequality:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {6 4}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {\text {i n i t}}}) ^ {4}} \left[ \left| \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} - \left(\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2}\right) \right| \cdot \left| \beta_ {W _ {L: 1}} \right| \right. \tag {70} \\ \left. + \left| \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) ^ {2} \right| \cdot \left| \beta_ {W _ {L: 1}} - \beta_ {W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}} \right| \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

We treat each of the four terms separately. From Equation (67) in Lemma 27 we know that:

$$
\left| \sigma_ {1} (W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} (W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} - (\sigma_ {1} (W _ {L: 1}) ^ {2} - \sigma_ {2} (W _ {L: 1}) ^ {2} \right| \leq 2 L ^ {2} (R + 1) ^ {2 L - 1} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}.
$$

Since  $|\operatorname{det}(W_{L:1})| = \sigma_1(W_{L:1}) \cdot \sigma_2(W_{L:1}) \leq \| W_{L:1} \|_F^2$ , the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and Equation (65) from Lemma 27 give:

$$
\left| \beta_ {W _ {L: 1}} \right| \leq \left\| W _ {L: 1} \right\| _ {F} ^ {3} \left\| \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\| _ {F} \leq 2 L (R + 1) ^ {6 L - 2}.
$$

Furthermore,  $\left|\sigma_{1}(W_{L:1})^{2} - \sigma_{2}(W_{L:1})^{2}\right| \leq \| W_{L:1}\|_{F}^{2} \leq (R + 1)^{2L}$ . The remaining term is  $\left|\beta_{W_{L:1}} - \beta_{W_{L:1}^{\prime}}\right|$ . Adding and subtracting  $\operatorname*{det}(W_{L:1}^{\prime})\left\langle A_{W_{L:1}},\frac{d}{dt} W_{L:1}\right\rangle$ , it can be upper bounded by:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \det  \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) - \det  \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) \right| \left| \left\langle A _ {W _ {L: 1}}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\rangle \right| \\ + \left| \det  \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) \right| \left| \left\langle A _ {W _ {L: 1}}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} \right\rangle - \left\langle A _ {W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}}, \frac {d}{d t} W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\rangle \right|. \\ \end{array}
$$

From Lemma 28 we have that  $\left|\left\langle A_{W_{L:1}}, \frac{d}{dt} W_{L:1}\right\rangle - \left\langle A_{W_{L:1}','} \frac{d}{dt} W_{L:1}'\right\rangle \right| \leq 6L^3 (R + 1)^{4L - 3} \cdot \sqrt{\epsilon}$ . Furthermore, Theorem 2.12 in [42] implies that:

$$
\left| \det  \left(W _ {L: 1}\right) - \det  \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) \right| \leq 2 \| W _ {L: 1} - W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \| _ {F} \max  \left\{\| W _ {L: 1} \| _ {F}, \| W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \| _ {F} \right\}.
$$

Thus, with the use of Equations (60) and (65), we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \beta_ {W _ {L: 1}} - \beta_ {W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}} \right| \leq 2 L (R + 1) ^ {4 L - 2} \cdot | \det  (W _ {L: 1}) - \det  (W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}) | + 6 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {6 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} \\ \leq 2 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {6 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} + 6 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {6 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} \\ = 8 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {6 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Put together, the expression in Equation (70) is upper bounded by:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {6 4}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {4}} \left[ 4 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {8 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} + 8 L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {8 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} \right] \\ = \frac {7 6 8}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {4}} L ^ {3} (R + 1) ^ {8 L - 3} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/9da5605109800a8c0bcfd4cbc80f7e98a8213cae980155ab8d1a63f36dc8b790.jpg)

# D.13.2 Proof of Equations (22), (23) and (24) (if weight matrices are not bounded by  $R$ )

Assume that there exists  $t \in [0, T]$  with  $\max_{l \in [L]} \| W_l(t) \|_F > R$ . We examine the initial time at which the Frobenius norm of one of the weight matrices reaches  $R$ . Formally, define:

$$
\bar {t} := \inf  \left\{\hat {t} | \hat {t} \in [ 0, t ] \text {a n d} \exists l \in [ L ] \text {s . t .} \| W _ {l} (\hat {t}) \| _ {F} = R \right\}.
$$

Since  $R \geq \max_{l \in [L]} \| W_l(0) \|_F$  — implied by the assumption on  $\epsilon$  — and  $W_1(t), W_2(t), \ldots, W_L(t)$  are continuous functions of  $t$ , the set on the right-hand side is non-empty and compact. Hence,  $\bar{t}$  is well defined (i.e.,  $-\infty < \bar{t} < \infty$ ), with  $\max_{l \in [L]} \| W_l(\bar{t}) \|_F = R$ .

Next, we derive a lower bound on  $|(W_{L:1}(\bar{t}))_{1,1}|$  — the absolute value of the unobserved entry. According to Lemmas 3 and 1, there exist balanced matrices  $W_1', W_2', \ldots, W_L'$  satisfying:

$$
\left\| W _ {l} (\bar {t}) - W _ {l} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} \leq (l - 1) \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}, \forall l \in [ L ]. \tag {71}
$$

Applying Lemma 12, and noticing that  $\max_{l\in [L]}\| W_l'\| _F\leq R + (L - 1)\cdot \sqrt{\epsilon}\leq R + 1$ , we bound the distance between the induced product matrices:

$$
\left\| W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t}) - W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} \leq (R + 1) ^ {L - 1} \cdot \sum_ {l = 1} ^ {L} (l - 1) \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} \leq \frac {1}{2} L ^ {2} (R + 1) ^ {L - 1} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}. \tag {72}
$$

Since  $W_1', W_2', \ldots, W_L'$  are balanced, they have the same singular values. In particular, this means that  $\| W_l' \|_F = \| W_{l'}' \|_F$  for any  $l, l' \in [L]$ . Additionally, by Lemma 8 we know that  $\sigma_1(W_{L:1}') = \sigma_1(W_1')^L$ . Thus:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {F} \geq \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime}\right) \\ = \sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {1} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {L} \\ \geq \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {2}} \| W _ {1} ^ {\prime} \| _ {F}\right) ^ {L} \\ = 2 ^ {- \frac {L}{2}} \max _ {l \in [ L ]} \| W _ {l} ^ {\prime} \| _ {F} ^ {L} \\ \geq 2 ^ {- \frac {L}{2}} \cdot \left(R - (L - 1) \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}\right) ^ {L}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last transition is by Equation (71) and the fact that  $\max_{l\in [L]}\| W_l(\bar{t})\| _F = R$ . The inequality above, combined with Equation (72), leads to:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t}) \| _ {F} \geq \| W _ {L: 1} ^ {\prime} \| _ {F} - \frac {1}{2} L ^ {2} (R + 1) ^ {L - 1} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon} \\ \geq 2 ^ {- \frac {L}{2}} \cdot (R - (L - 1) \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}) ^ {L} - \frac {1}{2} L ^ {2} (R + 1) ^ {L - 1} \cdot \sqrt {\epsilon}. \\ \end{array}
$$

From the definition of  $R$  it holds that  $\epsilon \leq \min \left\{\left(\frac{(1 - 1 / \sqrt{2})\cdot R}{L - 1}\right)^2,\frac{(R / 2)^{2L}}{L^4(R + 1)^{2L - 2}}\right\}$ , and therefore:

$$
\left\| W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t}) \right\| _ {F} \geq \left(\frac {R}{2}\right) ^ {L} - \frac {1}{2} \left(\frac {R}{2}\right) ^ {L} = \frac {R ^ {L}}{2 ^ {L + 1}}. \tag {73}
$$

Recall that  $\ell_{init} < \ell(0) = 1$ . The loss during gradient flow is monotonically non-increasing with respect to time (Lemma 17), hence,  $\ell(W_{L:1}(t)) < 1$ . This leads to the following bounds on the observed entries:

$$
\left| \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right) _ {1, 2} \right| \leq 1 + \sqrt {2}, \quad \left| \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right) _ {2, 1} \right| \leq 1 + \sqrt {2}, \quad \left| \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right) _ {2, 2} \right| \leq \sqrt {2}. \tag {74}
$$

Applying these bounds, we obtain:

$$
\left\| W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t}) \right\| _ {F} \leq \left| \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right) _ {1, 1} \right| + \sqrt {2 (1 + \sqrt {2}) ^ {2} + 2} \leq \left| \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right) _ {1, 1} \right| + 4.
$$

Combined with Equation (73), we have that:

$$
\left| \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right) _ {1, 1} \right| \geq \frac {R ^ {L}}{2 ^ {L + 1}} - 4. \tag {75}
$$

We are now in a position to establish Equations (22), (23) and (24). Starting with Equation (22), for any quasi-norm  $\|\cdot\|$  it holds that:

$$
\left\| W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t}) \right\| \geq \frac {1}{c _ {\left\| \cdot \right\|}} \left\| \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right) _ {1, 1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} ^ {\top} \right\| - \left\| W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t}) - \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right) _ {1, 1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} ^ {\top} \right\|, \tag {76}
$$

where  $c_{\| \cdot \|} \geq 1$  is a constant for which  $\| \cdot \|$  satisfies the weakened triangle inequality (see Footnote 2). Subsequent applications of the weakened triangle inequality, together with homogeneity of  $\| \cdot \|$  and the bounds on the observed entries (Equation (74)), give:

$$
\left\| W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t}) - (W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})) _ {1, 1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} ^ {\top} \right\| \leq 8 c _ {\| \cdot \|} ^ {2} \max _ {i, j \in \{1, 2 \}} \left\| \mathbf {e} _ {i} \mathbf {e} _ {j} ^ {\top} \right\|.
$$

Plugging the inequality above into Equation (76), and lower bounding  $|(W_{L:1}(\bar{t}))_{1,1}|$  according to Equation (75), we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t}) \| \geq \frac {\left\| \mathbf {e} _ {1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} ^ {\top} \right\|}{c _ {\| \cdot \|}} \cdot | (W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})) _ {1, 1} | - 8 c _ {\| \cdot \|} ^ {2} \max  _ {i, j \in \{1, 2 \}} \left\| \mathbf {e} _ {i} \mathbf {e} _ {j} ^ {\top} \right\| \tag {77} \\ \geq \frac {\left\| \mathbf {e} _ {1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} ^ {\top} \right\|}{2 ^ {L + 1} c _ {\| \cdot \|}} \cdot R ^ {L} - 1 2 c _ {\| \cdot \|} ^ {2} \max  _ {i, j \in \{1, 2 \}} \left\| \mathbf {e} _ {i} \mathbf {e} _ {j} ^ {\top} \right\|. \\ \end{array}
$$

The assumption on the size of  $\epsilon$  implies that  $R\geq 32$ . Hence:

$$
R \geq \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \frac {1}{2} \left[ \frac {\left(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}\right) ^ {4}}{2 ^ {1 6}} \cdot \ln \left(\frac {1}{\epsilon}\right) \right] ^ {1 / 6}, & \text {i f d e p t h} L = 2 \\ \frac {1}{2} \left[ \frac {\left(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}\right) ^ {4}}{2 ^ {2 L + 8} L ^ {4}} \cdot \frac {1}{\epsilon^ {1 / 3 2}} \right] ^ {1 / (4 L - 2)}, & \text {i f d e p t h} L \geq 3 \end{array} . \right. \tag {78}
$$

Applying Equation (78) to Equation (77), we obtain:

$$
\| W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t}) \| \geq \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \frac {\left\| \mathbf {e} _ {1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} ^ {\top} \right\| (1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {4 / 3}}{2 ^ {3 1 / 3} c _ {\parallel \cdot \parallel}} \cdot \ln \left(\frac {1}{\epsilon}\right) ^ {1 / 3} - 1 2 c _ {\parallel \cdot \parallel} ^ {2} \max  _ {i, j \in \{1, 2 \}} \left\| \mathbf {e} _ {i} \mathbf {e} _ {j} ^ {\top} \right\| & , \text {i f d e p t h L = 2} \\ \frac {\left\| \mathbf {e} _ {1} \mathbf {e} _ {1} ^ {\top} \right\| (1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {\frac {2 L}{2 L - 1}}}{2 ^ {\frac {5 L ^ {2} + 4 L - 1}{2 L - 1}} L ^ {\frac {2 L}{2 L - 1}} c _ {\parallel \cdot \parallel}} \cdot \epsilon^ {- \frac {L}{1 2 8 L - 6 4}} - 1 2 c _ {\parallel \cdot \parallel} ^ {2} \max  _ {i, j \in \{1, 2 \}} \left\| \mathbf {e} _ {i} \mathbf {e} _ {j} ^ {\top} \right\| & , \text {i f d e p t h L \geq 3} \end{array} . \right.
$$

For clarity, we simplify the exponents in the lower bound above. In the case of  $L = 2$ , we use the fact that  $2^{31/3} \leq 2^{11}$ . For  $L \geq 3$ , noticing that  $2L / (2L - 1) \leq 6/5$  and  $(5L^2 + 4L - 1) / (2L - 1) \leq 4L$  completes the proof of Equation (22).

Next, we derive the upper bound for effective rank (Equation (23)). Let  $h(p) \coloneqq -p \cdot \ln(p) - (1 - p) \cdot \ln(1 - p)$  be the binary entropy function, defined over  $[0,1]$ . Recall that the effective rank of  $W_{L:1}(\bar{t})$  is defined to be  $\operatorname{erank}(W_{L:1}(\bar{t})) \coloneqq \exp\{h(\rho_2(W_{L:1}(\bar{t})))\}$ , where  $\rho_2(W_{L:1}(\bar{t})) \coloneqq \sigma_2(W_{L:1}(\bar{t})) / (\sigma_1(W_{L:1}(\bar{t})) + \sigma_2(W_{L:1}(\bar{t})))$ . Since the exponent function is convex, it is upper bounded on the interval  $[0, \ln(2)]$  by the linear function that intersects it at these points. That is:

$$
\operatorname {e r a n k} \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right) \leq 1 + \frac {1}{\ln (2)} \cdot h \left(\rho_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right)\right).
$$

From Lemma 9 we know that  $h(\rho_2(W_{L:1}(\bar{t})) \leq 2\sqrt{\rho_2(W_{L:1}(\bar{t}))}$ . Combined with the fact that  $\inf_{W' \in S} \operatorname{erank}(W') = 1$  (Proposition 2), this leads to the following upper bound:

$$
\operatorname {e r a n k} \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right) \leq \inf  _ {W ^ {\prime} \in \mathcal {S}} \operatorname {e r a n k} \left(W ^ {\prime}\right) + \frac {2}{\ln (2)} \cdot \sqrt {\rho_ {2} \left(W _ {L : 1} (\bar {t})\right)}. \tag {79}
$$

We now lower bound  $\rho_{1}(W_{L:1}(\bar{t}))\coloneqq \sigma_{1}(W_{L:1}(\bar{t})) / (\sigma_{1}(W_{L:1}(\bar{t})) + \sigma_{2}(W_{L:1}(\bar{t})))$  as follows:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \rho_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right) \geq \frac {\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L : 1}\right)}{\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L : 1}\right) + 2 ^ {L + 2} / R ^ {L} + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1} (\bar {t})\right)}} \\ = 1 - \frac {2 ^ {L + 2} / R ^ {L} + \sqrt {2 \ell (W _ {L : 1} (\bar {t}))}}{\sigma_ {1} (W _ {L : 1}) + 2 ^ {L + 2} / R ^ {L} + \sqrt {2 \ell (W _ {L : 1} (\bar {t}))}} \\ \geq 1 - \frac {2 ^ {L + 2} / R ^ {L} + \sqrt {2 \ell (W _ {L : 1} (\bar {t}))}}{R ^ {L} / 2 ^ {L + 2} + 2 ^ {L + 2} / R ^ {L}} \\ \geq 1 - \frac {2 ^ {L + 2} / R ^ {L} + \sqrt {2 \ell (W _ {L : 1} (\bar {t}))}}{R ^ {L} / 2 ^ {L + 2}} \\ = 1 - \left(\frac {2 ^ {L + 2}}{R ^ {L}}\right) ^ {2} - \sqrt {2 \ell (W _ {L : 1} (\bar {t}))} \cdot \frac {2 ^ {L + 2}}{R ^ {L}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first and second inequalities are due to Lemma 31. Since  $2^{L + 2} / R^L < 1$  and  $\ell (W_{L:1}(\bar{t})) < 1$ , it holds that  $\rho_1(W_{L:1}(\bar{t}))\geq 1 - 2^{L + 4} / R^L$ , or equivalently,  $\rho_{2}(W_{L:1}(\bar{t}))\leq 2^{L + 4} / R^{L}$ . Going back to Equation 79, we have:

$$
\operatorname {e r a n k} \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right) \leq \inf  _ {W ^ {\prime} \in \mathcal {S}} \operatorname {e r a n k} \left(W ^ {\prime}\right) + \frac {2 ^ {L / 2 + 3}}{\ln (2) \cdot R ^ {L / 2}}.
$$

Applying the lower bound on  $R$  from Equation (78), we arrive at:

$$
\operatorname {e r a n k} (W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})) \leq \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \inf  _ {W ^ {\prime} \in \mathcal {S}} \operatorname {e r a n k} (W ^ {\prime}) + \frac {2 ^ {2 3 / 3}}{\ln (2) (1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {2 / 3}} \cdot \ln \left(\frac {1}{\epsilon}\right) ^ {- 1 / 6} & , \text {i f d e p t h L = 2} \\ \inf  _ {W ^ {\prime} \in \mathcal {S}} \operatorname {e r a n k} (W ^ {\prime}) + \frac {2 ^ {\frac {5 L ^ {2} + 1 4 L - 6}{4 L - 2}} L ^ {\frac {L}{2 L - 1}}}{\ln (2) (1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {\frac {L}{2 L - 1}}} \cdot \epsilon^ {\frac {L}{2 5 6 L - 1 2 8}} & , \text {i f d e p t h L \geq 3} \end{array} \right..
$$

In the case of  $L = 2$ , we simplify the upper bound using the fact that  $2^{23/3} / \ln(2) \leq 2^9$ . For  $L \geq 3$ , noticing that  $L / (2L - 1) \leq 1$  and  $(5L^2 + 14L - 6) / (4L - 2) \leq 2L + 4$  establishes Equation (23).

Finally, we turn our attention to the upper bound for distance from infimal rank (Equation (24)). By Proposition 2, the infimal rank of  $S$  is 1. Therefore, Lemma 31 yields:

$$
D \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t}), \mathcal {M} _ {1}\right) = \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right) \leq \frac {2 ^ {L + 2}}{R ^ {L}} + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1} (\bar {t})\right)}.
$$

Applying the lower bound on  $R$  from Equation (78), we arrive at:

$$
D (W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t}), \mathcal {M} _ {\mathrm {i r a n k} (\mathcal {S})}) \leq \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \frac {2 ^ {3 4 / 3}}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {4 / 3}} \cdot \ln \left(\frac {1}{\epsilon}\right) ^ {- 1 / 3} + \sqrt {2 \ell (W _ {L : 1} (\bar {t}))} & , \text {i f d e p t h L = 2} \\ \frac {2 ^ {\frac {5 L ^ {2} + 6 L - 2}{2 L - 1}} L ^ {\frac {2 L}{2 L - 1}}}{(1 - \sqrt {\ell_ {i n i t}}) ^ {\frac {2 L}{2 L - 1}}} \cdot \epsilon^ {\frac {L}{1 2 8 L - 6 4}} + \sqrt {2 \ell (W _ {L : 1} (\bar {t}))} & , \text {i f d e p t h L \geq 3} \end{array} \right..
$$

In the case of  $L = 2$ , we simplify the upper bound using the fact that  $2^{34/3} \leq 2^{12}$ . For  $L \geq 3$  noticing that  $2L / (2L - 1) \leq 6/5$  and  $(5L^2 + 6L - 2) / (2L - 1) \leq 3L + 4$  concludes the proof of Equation (24).

# D.13.2.1 Auxiliary Lemma

Lemma 31. In the context of the proof for Equations (22), (23) and (24) (Subappendix D.13.2), the singular values of  $W_{L:1}(\bar{t})$  fulfill:

$$
\sigma_ {1} \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right) \geq \frac {R ^ {L}}{2 ^ {L + 2}} - \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1} (\bar {t})\right)} \quad , \quad \sigma_ {2} \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right) \leq \frac {2 ^ {L + 2}}{R ^ {L}} + \sqrt {2 \ell \left(W _ {L : 1} (\bar {t})\right)}. \tag {80}
$$

Proof. Define  $W_{\mathcal{S}} \coloneqq \left( \begin{array}{cc}(W_{L:1}(\bar{t}))_{1,1} & 1\\ 1 & 0 \end{array} \right)$ , the orthogonal projection of  $W_{L:1}(\bar{t})$  onto the solution set  $\mathcal{S}$  (Equation (7)). Suppose that  $(W_{L:1}(\bar{t}))_{1,1} \geq 0$ . The largest singular value of  $W_{\mathcal{S}}$  can be written as:

$$
\sigma_ {1} (W _ {\mathcal {S}}) = \max  _ {i = 1, 2} | \lambda_ {i} (W _ {\mathcal {S}}) | = \frac {1}{2} \left((W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})) _ {1, 1} + \sqrt {(W _ {L : 1} (\bar {t})) _ {1 , 1} ^ {2} + 4}\right).
$$

Thus, lower bounding  $(W_{L:1}(\bar{t}))_{1,1}$  with Equation (75), and noticing that  $(W_{L:1}(\bar{t}))_{1,1}^2 + 4 \geq 16$ , leads to  $\sigma_1(W_S) \geq R^L / 2^{L+2}$ . Analogously, for the smallest singular value of  $W_S$  we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {2} (W _ {\mathcal {S}}) = \min  _ {i = 1, 2} | \lambda_ {i} (W _ {\mathcal {S}}) | \\ = \frac {1}{2} \left(\sqrt {\left(W _ {L : 1} (\bar {t})\right) _ {1 , 1} ^ {2} + 4} - \left(W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})\right) _ {1, 1}\right) \\ = \frac {2}{\sqrt {\left(W _ {L : 1} (\bar {t})\right) _ {1 , 1} ^ {2} + 4} + \left(W _ {L : 1} (\bar {t})\right) _ {1 , 1}} \\ \leq 2 ^ {L + 2} / R ^ {L}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the third transition we made use of the identity  $a - b = \frac{a^2 - b^2}{a + b}$  for  $a, b \in \mathbb{R}$  such that  $a + b \neq 0$ . Corollary 8.6.2 in [33] yields the following singular values perturbation bound:

$$
\left| \sigma_ {i} (W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t})) - \sigma_ {i} (W _ {\mathcal {S}}) \right| \leq \left\| W _ {L: 1} (\bar {t}) - W _ {\mathcal {S}} \right\| _ {F}, i = 1, 2.
$$

Equation (80) then readily follows from the fact that  $\| W_{L:1}(\bar{t}) - W_S\| _F = \sqrt{2\ell(W_{L:1}(t))}$ .

By similar arguments, Equation (80) holds when  $(W_{L:1}(\bar{t}))_{1,1} < 0$  as well.
